========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I, um, I hope I'm not wasting your time. My daughter actually suggested I make an appointment... 

I suppose I've just been noticing that I'm... well, I feel like I’m always up and moving. Which is a good thing at my age, right? Just keeping active? But, um, even on a quiet day at home, I find myself sort of bouncing from sorting out the garden club list, to my knitting bag, and then over to looking at some travel papers... usually before I’ve really finished any one of those things. 

It's really not a terrible problem, I don't think, just a little quirk of mine to stay busy! But my family thought I should mention it to you. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question properly.

Oh, I think... well, like I might have mentioned just a little bit ago, no, definitely not. If I happen to miss some sleep, even by accident, oh my, I certainly feel it the next day! ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I do try my very best to pay attention, I really do. I would never want anyone to think I'm being rude or ignoring them! But... oh, I suppose sometimes my mind does wander just a little bit. 

It's just that if, say, someone is talking in the next room, or... or even if just a car goes by the window outside, my mind just jumps right over there to whatever that noise was. And then, before I know it, I completely lose what I was doing or what we were talking about. 

I get so embarrassed when it happens. I hope you don't think I'm just being scatterbrained or not listening on purpose? I really do try to stay focused and be a good listener. It's just those little noises that seem to catch me off guard. That's not too terrible, is it?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... I feel terrible, but I really must be giving you the wrong impression if you have to keep asking me this. I promise, I'm not hiding anything from you. My mood hasn't been like that at all.

Are my answers alright so far?\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed yourself doing things much more quickly than usual, or feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 70 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Feeling_Restless\nmanifestation: \"If I have to sit still for too long, I get this stirred-up feeling inside like I need to stand up and straighten something or go check on something.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I wouldn't say I do things unusually fast, no. I like to take my time and do things properly. But, um... I suppose I do sometimes find it a little tricky to just sit in one place for a very long stretch. 

It's not that I'm pacing the floors or anything disruptive like that! It's just... if I'm sitting down for too long, say watching a program or something, I get this sort of... stirred-up feeling inside? Like a little flutter that makes me feel like I ought to stand up and just, oh, straighten a picture frame or go check if I left a light on in the hallway. 

I just like to keep things tidy and make sure everything is in order, you know? It's nothing terribly bothersome, just a little urge to be useful, I suppose. Is that... is that the sort of thing you were wondering about? I hope that makes sense. I don't want to make it sound like I'm bouncing off the walls or anything silly like that.

My daughter actually suggested I make an appointment... \n\nI suppose I've just been noticing that I'm... well, I feel like I’m always up and moving. Which is a good thing at my age, right? Just keeping active? But, um, even on a quiet day at home, I find myself sort of bouncing from sorting out the garden club list, to my knitting bag, and then over to looking at some travel papers... usually before I’ve really finished any one of those things. \n\nIt's really not a terrible problem, I don't think, just a little quirk of mine to stay busy! But my family thought I should mention it to you. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling unusually upbeat, overly confident, or maybe even irritable lately, even for a few days at a time?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I don't think so. I mean, I try to be a cheerful person generally, but not... not unusually upbeat, if that makes sense? Just my normal self. And certainly not overly confident. Oh my, no, I'm usually the one second-guessing myself! \n\nAnd as for being irritable... well, I really try my very best not to be a bother or snap at anyone. I like to keep things peaceful at home. So, no, I haven't noticed any spells like that at all. Is that... is that what you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need less sleep than usual lately, like feeling rested after only a few hours of sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I still need my full night's rest, just like always. I usually try to get my seven or eight hours, you know, to make sure I have enough energy for the day. \n\nI haven't had any nights where I'm just wide awake and ready to go after only a few hours. I think I'd be quite a grump if that happened! So, my sleep has been very normal, very steady. I hope that's a good thing? I just try to take good care of myself and stick to my routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure in your usual activities, like gardening, knitting, or looking at travel plans?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I still enjoy all of those things very much! I mean, I might bounce around between them a bit, like I mentioned, but I still love doing them. \n\nMy garden club is just a joy, and knitting... well, I've been knitting for years and it always brings a smile to my face. I certainly haven't lost any interest or pleasure in my hobbies. I like to stay positive and keep myself busy with things I love. Is that... does that sound alright? I hope I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you feel unusually tired or low on energy, even if you’ve had a good night’s sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens, no. I really try to keep my pep up, you know? I mean, of course, anyone might get a little worn out after a long afternoon of chores or being out in the garden, but... no, I don't have days where I just feel drained for no reason. \n\nIf I get my sleep, I'm quite ready to tackle the day! I always try to put my best foot forward and stay active. I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around the house feeling sluggish or letting myself go. I like to keep moving. Is that... I hope it's okay that I still have plenty of energy? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re able to function well on less sleep than usual, even if you didn’t intend to sleep less?\n\nPatient: Oh, I think... well, like I might have mentioned just a little bit ago, no, definitely not. If I happen to miss some sleep, even by accident, oh my, I certainly feel it the next day! I wouldn't be able to just breeze through my morning and function well at all. I think I'd be dragging my feet the whole time. \n\nI really try to be very responsible with my bedtime so I don't get all out of sorts. I hope that's the right way to go about it? I just really need my proper rest to be at my best, you know, so I'm not a bother to anyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood was unusually high, overly excited, or even irritable for most of the time, not just here and there?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I really try my best to keep an even keel, you know? I mean, I get happy about things, of course, but not in a way that's... well, too much. \n\nAnd irritable? Oh, I certainly hope I'm never like that! I really do try to be pleasant and agreeable. I wouldn't want to snap at anyone or make things difficult for the people around me by being in a bad mood all day. If I ever get a little flustered, maybe if I've misplaced something, it passes very quickly. I just try to stay steady and cheerful. Am I... I hope it's alright that I'm mostly just my usual self? I just want to make sure I'm answering these right for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately, like eating much less or losing weight without trying, or maybe eating more and gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I haven't noticed anything like that. My appetite is just fine, really. I always make sure to eat my three meals a day, just like you're supposed to. I wouldn't want to skip meals or overeat and let myself go—I try to be very good about my portions and taking care of my health. \n\nMy clothes all fit exactly the same as they always have, so my weight must be staying right where it should be. I do try to make nice, sensible choices when I cook. I hope that's what you like to hear? I just want to be sure I'm doing all the right things to take care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you get easily distracted or have trouble staying focused on tasks or conversations lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I do try my very best to pay attention, I really do. I would never want anyone to think I'm being rude or ignoring them! But... oh, I suppose sometimes my mind does wander just a little bit. \n\nIt's just that if, say, someone is talking in the next room, or... or even if just a car goes by the window outside, my mind just jumps right over there to whatever that noise was. And then, before I know it, I completely lose what I was doing or what we were talking about. \n\nI get so embarrassed when it happens. I hope you don't think I'm just being scatterbrained or not listening on purpose? I really do try to stay focused and be a good listener. It's just those little noises that seem to catch me off guard. That's not too terrible, is it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had several days in a row where your mood was noticeably more high, excited, or irritable than usual for most of the day—even if it didn’t last for a long period?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I really try to keep things steady, you know? I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm prone to outbursts or getting overly worked up about things. \n\nI mean, I might get a little flustered if I misplace my glasses, but that's just a moment or two. It certainly wouldn't last for days on end. I really do try to be agreeable and keep a pleasant mood. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently more upbeat, energetic, or different from your usual self for a number of days in a row lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I think... well, you just asked something very similar, didn't you? And it's still the same answer, I promise. I haven't been acting unusually upbeat or energetic, or anything like that. \n\nI just try to be my normal, cheerful self. I wouldn't want to be one of those people who are just bouncing off the walls or acting strangely out of the blue. I like to keep a nice, even keel. I hope I'm making sense? I really am just trying to be helpful and answer everything properly for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you had most days where your mood has been clearly elevated, unusually irritable, or more expansive than usual for most of the day, nearly every day, rather than just brief changes here and there?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I feel terrible, but I really must be giving you the wrong impression if you have to keep asking me this. I promise, I'm not hiding anything from you. My mood hasn't been like that at all. \n\nI really do just stay my usual self—maybe a little tired sometimes, but certainly not unusually elevated or irritable for days on end. I would hate for you to think I'm the sort of person who takes their frustrations out on others or acts out of sorts all day. I always try to be polite and pleasant. \n\nAm I... am I answering this wrong? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job for you. I truly haven't had any days like that, I swear it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself talking more than usual, or feeling like you need to keep talking, even when you don't necessarily want to?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I do enjoy a nice chat with my neighbors or the ladies at the grocery store, but I wouldn't say I talk *too* much. I always try to be a good listener, you know? I think it's so important to let other people have their turn to speak. \n\nI certainly wouldn't want to be one of those people who just talks someone's ear off and won't let them get a word in edgewise. That would be terribly rude, wouldn't it? I really try to be mindful of that. I hope that's what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you had most days where you felt down, sad, or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I haven't been depressed or sad like that at all. I mean, everyone has a slightly off day here or there, don't they? A rainy afternoon where you just feel a bit tired. But certainly not every day for two weeks. \n\nI always try to look on the bright side of things. It's so important to keep a positive attitude, I think. I wouldn't want to be a burden to anyone by moping around all the time. I like to keep busy and keep my chin up. \n\nI hope that's helpful? I'm really trying to give you the clearest answers I can. I just haven't felt anything like what you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had several days in a row—say, a week or more—where you felt unusually good, maybe even a bit 'high,' or more irritable than usual, for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really haven't. I... I know you have to ask all these things, and I hope I'm not frustrating you by just saying no again, but I truly don't have days like that. I'm just my normal self, really. \n\nI would feel just awful if I were irritable for a whole week and took it out on the people around me. That's just not how I try to behave at all. I always try to keep a nice, even temper and be pleasant to everyone I meet. \n\nI hope I'm giving you what you need... I'm trying my very best to be helpful for you. Are my answers alright so far?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself doing things much more quickly than usual, or feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I... Like a little flutter that makes me feel like I ought to stand up and just, oh, straighten a picture frame or go check if I left a light on in the hallway. \n\nI just like to keep things tidy and make sure everything is in order, you know? It's nothing terribly bothersome, just a little urge to be useful, I suppose. Is that... is that the sort of thing you were wondering about? I hope that makes sense. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness, no. I haven't ever had a time like that. Two whole weeks of feeling sad every single day... oh, that sounds just dreadful. 

I really do try to look on the bright side of things, you know? I think it's so important to count your blessings and keep a smile on, even when things are a little tough. I'd hate to be a burden to anyone by moping around for weeks on end. I just keep myself busy and try to stay positive. 

I've never had a depression like that. I hope that's a good thing to hear? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.
